2026. 10.27
先週（10/19～10/26）発表の主な経済指標
米生産活動は堅調。AI関連投資を背景にコンピューター・電子
製品や機械などがけん引。一方、データセンター建設に伴う需要
増加が、他産業の生産や建設活動を圧迫する兆候も
コア消費者物価は再び2％超え。特殊要因を除くサービス価格
も加速し、人件費転嫁の兆し。連合は2027年春闘で6％中心
を要求。中小企業の余力に課題も、物価上振れに注意
注目点② サービス物価の加速で重み増す2027年春闘戦線注目点①AI投資の恩恵拡大、副作用もじわり顕在化
総合PMIは57.6と高水準を維持。新規失業保険申
請件数も19.4万件と低水準で解雇の動きは引き続
き限定的。一方で、住宅着工件数や住宅建設業
者の景況感は悪化、高金利の影響が継続

耐久財製造業は、軍需やデータセンター（DC）増
設の旺盛な需要が、他の製品の生産を圧迫して
いると報告ベージュブック
（2026/10） テクノロジー企業がDC向け電気設備の買い占め
を試みたが、供給業者は他案件への供給を優先
し、これを拒否
機械製造業は、DC向けに鋼材在庫が大量に消
費されるなか、鉄鋼メーカーによる供給制限が他
の建設案件の遅延を招いていると指摘
テキサス州
見通し調査
（2026/9）
製造業では原材料コストが上昇。特に鋼材価格
が急上昇しており、その一因はDC需要とされるベージュブック
（2026/9） 一部の製造業がアルミニウム・銅・鋼材の供給不
足や調達期間の長期化を報告
一部の自動車メーカーはアルミニウムや一部部品
の供給不足で生産が幾分抑制されたと報告

ベージュブック
（2026/7）
米国：AI関連を中心に製造業生産は堅調も、資材・設備の需給ひっ迫が他産業に波及
 米国の製造業生産は、AI関連投資を背景にコンピューター・電子製品、一次金属、一般機械などが堅調
― 一方、非耐久財や家具など消費関連の生産は軟調に推移し、業種間のばらつきが拡大
 FRBは、データセンター建設に伴う鋼材・電気設備などの需要増加が、他の建設案件や生産活動に影響していると指摘
―AI投資の拡大が関連産業の生産を押し上げる一方、供給制約を通じて他産業の活動を圧迫する兆候も
― 当面はAI投資による成長押し上げ効果が期待される一方、供給制約や価格上昇を通じた負の影響にも注意が必要
4

日本：全国コアCPIは再び2％超え。基調的なサービス価格も伸び高まる
5
 9月の全国コアCPIは前年比＋2.1％（前月：同＋1.7％）と加速。2025年12月以来の2％超に
― 原油高・円安等のコスト上昇もあり、家事用消耗品や教養娯楽用品等を中心に伸び率が拡大
― 一般サービス価格も伸び加速。シルバーウィークの並びによる宿泊料や外食費の上昇などが主因
 特殊要因を除いた一般サービス価格もじわり上昇
― 変動の大きい宿泊費や私立高校無償化による押し下げを除いた基調的な一般サービス価格に上昇圧力。人件費増の
価格転嫁が進んでいる可能性を示唆

全国コアCPI CPI一般サービス価格の推移

### Page 7

日本：連合は2027年春闘で賃上げ要求を強化。高水準の賃上げ持続と物価への波及が焦点
6
 連合は、2027年春闘賃上げ要求の目安を「6％中心、5％以上」と発表
―5％以上の賃上げを最低目標として明確化。実質賃金1％上昇の定着をめざす方針
― 中小企業は大企業との格差是正に向け「6％以上」が目標。過去4年のインフレで実質賃金が低下した分の回復も要求
― また、新たに、2030年に2026年の賃金水準を15％程度上回る中期目標を設定。持続的な賃金上昇をめざす
 賃上げ余力は企業規模・業種で濃淡。中小企業等で高い「賃上げ5％の壁」
― なお、6％に近い賃上げが実現する場合、サービス価格への波及が強まり、基調的物価上昇率が2％を上回る可能性
連合・春闘基本構想の新旧比較 春闘賃上げ率

基本構想」 より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 8

欧州：2027年度予算審議が本格化。生活苦によるデモが拡大する中、財政赤字は高水準
7
 2027年度予算審議が本格化。財政赤字は総じて高止まり
― 一定の防衛費・エネルギー関連支出を柔軟化する特例措置の適用後も、複数の国でEU財政基準で定める赤字幅を超
過。スペインは予算案を巡って議会解散。フランス・ベルギーも10/15時点でEUに予算案を未提出
 生活改善を求める抗議活動が欧州各国で発生。支出圧力の高まりは財政再建の障壁に
― フランスの学生によるデモを発端に、生活費の高騰や公共サービスの劣化に対する抗議活動は欧州各国に拡大。一部
は暴動に発展し、警察官と衝突する事態に
9～10月に発生した主な抗議活動主要国の財政収支実績と見通し
（出所） 各種報道より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

欧州：金融市場は政治・財政を巡る混乱を嫌気。不透明感は2027年入り後も継続する見込み
8
 金融市場は政治・財政を巡る混乱を警戒。主要国の選挙を控える中、不透明感は2027年まで継続へ
― 抗議活動が広がるイタリア、フランス、ベルギーの一般政府債務残高（GDP比）は、EU加盟国の中でも上位（それぞれ2
位、3位、4位）。金利上昇の背景は政治・財政への懸念
― 影響は為替市場にも波及。9月中旬以降、ユーロはドルに対して弱含み
―2027年にかけてフランス大統領選やスペイン・イタリアの総選挙など、欧州主要国で重要な国政選挙が相次ぐ。極右・ポ

Document 037: 2026-11-16_［QE解説］7～9月期実質GDPは年率▲0.1％とほぼ横ばいの結果（1次QE）_714aa7e4

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-11-16_［QE解説］7～9月期実質GDPは年率▲0.1％とほぼ横ばいの結果（1次QE）_714aa7e4.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

1

7～9 月期実質GDP は年率▲0.1％と 
ほぼ横ばいの結果（1 次QE）

○ 2026年7～9月期の実質GDP（1次速報値）は前期比▲0.0％（年率▲0.1％）と、ほぼ横ばいの結果。
4～6月期（前期比＋0.4％）から減速し、経済の緩やかな拡大傾向が一服 
○ 個人消費が2四半期連続でマイナスとなったほか、輸出が一時的要因もあり小幅減となり、成長率
を下押し。一方、設備投資はAI関連需要の堅調さを背景にプラス転換し、成長率を押し上げ 
○ 年度後半の日本経済は、小幅なプラス成長を予想。インフレ再加速を受け消費抑制が続く一方、設
備投資が堅調に拡大。日本銀行は12月会合で政策金利を1.50％に引き上げる見込み

7～9月期の実質GDP（1次
速報値）は、ほぼ横ばい 
内閣府が本日公表した2026年7～9月期の「四半期別GDP速報（QE）」（ 1次
速報値）では、実質GDPが前期比▲0.0％（年率▲0.1％）と、ほぼ横ばいの
結果になった。4～6月期の前期比＋0.4％（年率＋1.7％）から減速し、経済
の緩やかな拡大傾向が一服した格好だ（図表）。 
需要項目別にみると、内需の柱である個人消費の減少や、輸出の小幅減少
が成長率の下押し要因になった。一方、設備投資は堅調に拡大し、成長率を
押し上げた。その他では、原油の代替調達進展を反映した輸入の増加がマイ
ナス要因になったが、前期の備蓄原油放出の反動が公的在庫投資のプラス要
因として現れ、両者の成長率に対する影響は概ね打ち消し合っている。以下、
需要項目別に7～9月期の経済動向を詳しく確認しよう。

### Page 2

2

個人消費は2四半期連続
で減少。根強い節約志向
や、耐久財の特需剥落が
影響 
個人消費は前期比▲0.3％と、2四半期連続で減少した（4～6月期は同＋
0.0％から同▲0.3％に下方修正）。足元の消費の弱さの背景には、複合的な
要因があると考えられる。 
品目別の内訳をみると、衣料品などの半耐久財が前期比▲1.4％（4～6月
期：同＋0.4％）と、大幅に減少した。長雨などの天候不順の影響で、衣料
品の消費が弱含んだとみられる。 
食品・エネルギー・各種消耗品などの非耐久財は、前期比▲0.2％（ 4～6月
期：同▲1.9％ ）と 2四半期連続で減少した。物価高を背景とする根強い節約
志向が影響したようだ。今回のインフレ局面が本格化する前の2020年と比較
すると、必需品が多い非耐久消費財の物価は足元までの約6年半でおよそ1.3
倍の水準に上昇した計算になる。こうした累積的な価格上昇が、消費者の体
感物価の高まりを通じて節約志向を強め、購入数量抑制につながっている可
能性がある。 
また、耐久財が前期比▲3.5％（4～6月期：同＋3.6％）と大幅減に転じた
ことも、7～9月期の消費を下押しした。これは、自動車とエアコンの「特需」
剥落が原因だ。自動車は、購入時にかかる自動車税の環境性能割が3月末に
廃止されたことを受け、4月から自動車販売が過去平均に比べ上振れていた
が、8月以降は再び過去平均並みの水準に戻っている。エアコンは、2027年
4月の省エネ基準引き上げで販売価格が上昇するとの見方から駆け込み需要
が生じていたが、景気ウォッチャー調査のコメントなどをみると、8月に入
った後は一服した模様だ。9月は気温が低かったほか、長雨による客足の減
少もエアコンなど季節家電の売り上げに悪影響を及ぼしたとみられる。 
サービス消費は前期比＋0.1％（4～6月期：同▲0.1％）とプラスに転じた
が、伸びは緩慢だ。イベント関連支出などで底堅さがみられる一方、日常的
な支出には物価高による節約志向が波及し、サービスや価格帯、顧客層によ
る二極化が進みつつあるとみられる。また、8～9月には台風・長雨による旅
行・宿泊のキャンセルや外出控えの影響から、レジャー関連を中心に消費が
伸び悩んだようだ。 
設備投資は3四半期ぶり
に前期比プラス 
一方、設備投資は前期比＋1.6％と、3四半期ぶりに前期比プラスへ転じた。
4～6月期も同▲0.9％から同▲0.4％と、依然マイナスながらも上方修正され
た。 
設備投資の関連指標では、資本財の国内向け供給量を表す資本財総供給指
数（除く輸送機械）や、第3次産業活動指数におけるソフトウェア業の実質
売上高が改善している。世界的なAIブームを背景とするデータセンター等の
AI関連投資や、人手不足を受けたソフトウェア導入などの省力化投資が影響
したとみられる。 
ただし、7～9月期の変化率には、前期発生した一時的な押し下げ圧力（特

### Page 3

3

許権の海外譲渡による国内研究開発投資の減少）の反動も影響している。足
元の設備投資の伸びは実態よりもやや強い結果とみられ、多少割り引いて評
価する必要があろう。 
住宅投資は前期比マイナ
スが継続 
住宅投資は前期比▲1.1％（4～6月期：同▲0.6％ ）と 、2四半期連続で前期
比マイナスになった。中東情勢を背景とする資材不足は徐々に緩和している
とみられるが、7～9月の住宅着工件数が伸び悩んでおり、住宅価格や金利上
昇による需要低迷に加えて、地震や台風などによる着工・進捗の遅れが発生
した可能性がある。 
民間在庫は成長率へのプ
ラス寄与が縮小 
民間在庫変動の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度は、±0.0％Pt（4～6月期：
＋0.3％Pt）とプラス幅が縮小した。4～6月期は石油化学製品などの供給不
足を懸念した前倒し発注が在庫を押し上げたとみられるが、7～9月期は原油
の代替調達進展に伴って供給不足が緩和したことで、そうした要因の影響が
一巡した模様だ。 
公的需要は備蓄原油放出
の反動で前期比プラス 
公的需要は前期比＋1.8％（4～6月期：同▲0.9％）と、2四半期ぶりに前期
比プラスとなった。 
最大の要因は、公的在庫変動の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度がプラスに
転じたことだ。4～6月期は国家備蓄原油の放出により公的在庫変動の寄与度
が▲0.5％Ptと大幅マイナスになったが、7～9月期は国家原油備蓄の減少が
小幅なものにとどまり（6月末：105日分→9月末：102日分）、公的在庫の減
少ペースが緩和することで、成長率に対し＋0.5％Ptとプラス寄与した。 
一方、その他の公的需要項目では、公共投資が前期比▲1.1％（4～6月期：
同▲0.6％）と2四半期連続のマイナスになった。台風等により土木関連工事
の進捗に遅れが発生した可能性がある。また、政府消費は前期比▲0.0％（ 4
～6月期：同＋1.5％）とほぼ横ばいにとどまった。4～6月期は高校授業料・
小学校給食費の無償化開始により高い伸びになったが、7～9月期はそうした
特殊要因が剥落した。 
原油代替調達で輸入が大
幅に増加し、外需はマイ
ナス寄与に反転 
外需では、輸出が前期比▲0.2％（4～6月期：同＋1.6％）と小幅に減少し
た一方、輸入が同＋2.5％（4～6月期：同▲1.3％）と大幅に増加した。その
結果、実質GDP成長率に対する外需の寄与度は▲0.6％Pt（4～6月期：同＋
0.6％Pt）と、マイナスに反転した。 
輸入の大幅な増加は、原油の代替調達進展が主因だ。中東情勢の緊迫化に
より4～6月期は一時的に原油輸入が急減したが、7～9月期は米国産原油やホ
ルムズ海峡を通過しない中東産原油の調達が増え、9月を除いて前年並みの
輸入量が確保されている。 
こうした輸入の大幅増加は、7～9月期のGDP成長率に対し▲0.5％Ptのマイ
ナス寄与になった。もっとも、先述したように代替調達の進展に伴って国家
備蓄原油の放出が抑制され、公的在庫変動が＋0.5％Ptのプラス寄与になっ

### Page 4

4

ており、輸入増による成長率押し下げ効果は公的在庫変動によりほぼ相殺さ
れた。 
輸出は、米国関税影響で落ち込んだ2025年7～9月期以来、4四半期ぶりの
前期比マイナスになった。内訳をみると、サービス輸出が前期比▲7.1％（ 4
～6月期：同＋8.9％ ）と 急減したことが主因だ。前期の4～6月期に特許権の
海外譲渡がサービス輸出を押し上げたが、7～9月期はその反動減が現れたた
めであり（これは先述した7～9月期の設備投資の押し上げ要因でもある）、
一時的な動きと言えよう。なお、インバウンド需要（非居住者家計の国内で
の直接購入）は、前期比▲2.4％（4～6月期＋1.6％）と減少した。日中対立
の継続による中国人訪日客の少なさや、航空運賃上昇を受けた欧米訪日客の
伸び悩みが影響したとみられる。 
一方、財輸出は前期比＋2.4％（ 4～6月期：同▲0.8％）と堅調に回復した。
旺盛なAI関連需要を背景に、半導体・電子部品や半導体製造装置などの財輸
出がけん引したようだ。今後はサービス輸出の特殊要因が剥落するなか、財
輸出の拡大傾向が続き、輸出は全体として増加基調をたどると考えられる。 
2026 年度後半の実質GDP
は小幅プラスと予想。個
人消費が伸び悩む一方、
設備投資が堅調拡大 
以上のとおり、7～9月期の日本経済は前期のプラス成長から減速し、ほぼ
横ばいの結果になった。先行きについてみると、2026年度後半（2026年10～
2027年3月）の実質GDPは、均してみれば小幅なプラス成長になると予想する。
個人消費が精彩を欠く一方で、設備投資の拡大が支えになる構図が続くだろ
う。2026年度全体の実質GDP成長率は、前年度末の堅調な伸びで今年度の平
均水準が押し上げられることもあり（いわゆるプラスのゲタ）、前年比＋1％
弱とまずまずの成長率になる計算だ。 
年度後半の個人消費では、インフレ再加速による実質賃金の伸び悩みが消
費拡大の抑制要因になるだろう。原油・ナフサ価格高騰の影響が徐々に波及
し、コア消費者物価指数（生鮮食品を除く総合）は前年比＋2％台後半まで
上昇すると予想される（電気・ガス料金支援の再開と、ガソリン補助金縮小
を考慮）。また、2027年4月の消費税減税開始前には、保存食品などを中心
とした買い控えの動きが生じるとみられ、一時的に消費の抑制要因になろ
う。ただし、2026年12月～2027年2月に政府の電気・ガス料金支援が実施さ
れることで、2027年1～3月期を中心に消費が下支えされる見込みだ。 
年度後半の設備投資は堅調に拡大するだろう。日銀短観（9月調査）では、
2026年度の設備投資計画（ソフトウェア・研究開発を含む設備投資額（除く
土地投資額））が前年比＋9.3％と高い伸びを維持した（6月調査：同＋8.8％ ）。
生産・営業用設備投資判断DIが2025年度後半以降に低下し、設備の不足感が
強まっていることをあわせて考えれば、設備投資は今後拡大傾向で推移して
いく見込みだ。 
また、政府の投資支援策である「大胆な投資促進税制」（特定生産性向上

### Page 5

5

設備等投資促進税制）が2026年7月末から開始されたことも、今後の設備投
資にとってプラス要因になろう。同税制における減税規模の政府見込みを参
考に試算すると、2026～2028年度に設備投資の前年比変化率を約0.1％Ptず
つ押し上げる効果があると考えられる。 
ただし、日銀が来年にかけて政策金利を一段と引き上げ、企業の新規借入
金利がさらに上昇すると予想されること、また、投資財の価格が上昇傾向を
続けると考えられることを踏まえると、中小企業を中心に設備投資を控える
企業が今後増加する可能性がある点には注意が必要だ。 
日銀の10月会合は、物価
目標到達を確認した歴史
的な日に。今後は、12月会
合で追加利上げを行う見
込み 
10月29・30日に開催された金融政策決定会合において、日本銀行は政策金
利を現行の1.25％で据え置いた。事前に報じられていたように、今回会合で
日銀は「消費者物価の基調的な上昇率は2％の「物価安定の目標」と概ね整
合的な水準」との見解を示した。2％の「物価安定の目標」を定めた2013年
1月から数えて、13年9カ月でようやく物価目標に到達した格好だ。これまで
長きにわたり実施してきたデフレ脱却のための特別な金融政策が、ついに終
わりを迎えたという意味で、歴史的な日になったと言える。 
今後は、基調的なインフレ率を2％程度の水準で「定着」させるために、物
価上振れ・下振れのリスクを都度評価して金融政策を調整していくことにな
る。当面は、中東情勢、AI関連需要、為替相場の変動（円安圧力）などを背
景に物価上振れリスクが強い状況が続くと見込まれ、日銀は金融環境への影
響を確認しながら利上げを継続するだろう。 
次の利上げ（1.25％→1.50％）は、次回12月17・18日会合と予想する。前
回利上げを行った9月会合に続いて「3カ月に1回」ペースでの利上げになり、
日銀の利上げペースが6月以前の「半年強に1回」から加速していることを印
象付けるものになろう。最終的には、2027年半ばにかけて政策金利を2.0％
まで引き上げていくとみている。 
なお、経済見通しの詳細については、みずほ総合研究所調査部「2026年・
2027年 みずほ世界経済見通し ─ 転換点は近いのか、投資主導経済の行方
を探る ─」（10月2日）をご覧いただきたい。 
（https://www.mizuhobank.co.jp/corporate/mhri/research/report/pdf/outlook261002.pdf）

### Page 1

兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
中華民國一一五年十月十二日

國際經濟金融週報

### Page 2

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

1

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
經濟 
⚫ 9 月美國 ISM 服務業 PMI 由 55.4 降至 54.9，連續第 27 個月維持擴張。
商業活動及新訂單雖較前月回落，仍保持穩健成長；就業重返擴張區間，
內需與勞動市場仍具韌性。惟價格指數續升創高，出口與進口動能轉弱，
後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化。 
⚫ 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元，較上月增加 13.7%。
出口 3,152 億美元、進口 4,208 億美元，分別月增 1.4%及 4.3%。出口成
長主要受非貨幣黃金、原油及半導體帶動，進口則以原油、黃金及資本
財增加為主。累計前 8 月貿易逆差 5,570 億美元，較去年同期縮減 19.9%，
惟近期進口增幅高於出口，使逆差再度擴大。 
⚫ 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示，AI 投資持續帶動經濟與就業動能，勞動市
場仍具韌性；惟去通膨進展有限，核心服務通膨及成本轉嫁壓力仍存。
全體官員支持升息 1 碼，傾向維持限制性政策，並關注 AI 融資、公債
市場波動及網路安全等金融風險。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 近期美伊緊張情勢再升溫，川普改口不排除選前對伊朗採取軍事行動，
伊朗則持續尋求重啟談判；同時葉門青年運動頻繁襲擊沙國目標，加劇
中東安全風險。若衝突擴大，恐影響荷姆茲與曼德海峽能源運輸，推升
油氣價格並增添全球經濟下行風險。 
⚫ 美國啟動 337 調查，針對 AI 伺服器垂直供電系統（VPD）相關專利侵
權爭議展開審查，鴻海、台達電、廣達等約 20 家業者列名。由於涉及
AI 伺服器關鍵供電技術，若最終認定侵權，相關產品恐面臨輸美限制，
對供應鏈營運帶來影響。 
短評 
⚫ 美國服務業與內需仍具韌性，AI 投資亦支撐經濟動能，惟貿易逆差擴
大、成本壓力升高及通膨黏著，使聯準會仍需維持偏緊縮立場。加上美
伊與中東局勢升溫，能源價格與金融市場波動風險上升，後續景氣不確
定性仍高。

一、 美國—美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
2026 年 9 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI)為 54.9，較上月
55.4 下降 0.5 個百分點，已連續第 27 個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，且高於近 12
個月平均值 54.1，服務業景氣仍持續穩健擴張。細項觀察，商業活動指數由 61.7
降至 56.5，新訂單指數由 60.9 降至 59.8，雖較前月回落，但仍維持高檔擴張；就
業指數則由 47.8 回升至 50.1，三個月來首度重返擴張區間。價格指數由 72.6 升
至 74.0，創 2022 年 7 月以來新高。供應商交貨指數由 51.3 升至 53.2，交貨時間
再度延長；庫存指數由 56.7 升至 57.8，企業持續提前備貨以因應未來成本上升風
險。此外，未完成訂單指數升至 56.6，創 2022 年 7 月以來高點，惟新出口訂單
指數由 56.3 大幅降至 46.9，八個月來首度跌入收縮區間，進口指數亦由 56.3 降
至 52.9，外部需求及採購動能均降溫。整體而言，美國服務業景氣仍維持穩健擴
張，內需與就業展現一定韌性，但燃料價格上漲、關稅政策、供應鏈限制及地緣
政治等因素持續推升企業成本，後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化對景氣的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元，較上月修正後 928
億美元增加 127 億美元，增幅 13.7%。當月出口 3,152 億美元，較上月增加 45 億
美元(+1.4%)；進口 4,208 億美元，較上月增加 172 億美元(+4.3%)。其中，商品貿
易逆差擴大至 1,366 億美元，服務貿易順差則小幅增至 310 億美元。出口成長主
要受工業用品及原材料出口增加 63 億美元帶動，其中非貨幣黃金、原油及燃油
出口增幅較為顯著，資本財出口亦受半導體、電腦及電腦周邊設備需求增加所支
撐；進口方面，工業用品及原材料進口增加 91 億美元，資本財進口增加 62 億美
元，其中原油、非貨幣黃金、半導體及工業機械進口增幅較大。累計 2026 年 1 至
8 月，美國商品及服務貿易逆差為 5,570 億美元，較 2025 年同期減少 1,382 億美
元，縮減 19.9%；同期出口成長 11.8%，進口增加 4.4%，顯示美國對外貿易失衡
情況較去年同期有所改善，惟近期進口增幅明顯高於出口，使貿易逆差再度呈現
擴大趨勢。 
2026 年 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要重點如下：(1)經濟：若干官員認為美國經濟成
長動能有所增強，AI 投資規模與速度持續超乎預期，帶動企業投資與生產力提升；

企業活動則受到企業獲利維持高檔及部分政策因素支持；(2)就業：多數官員表示
勞動市場近期略有改善，就業成長維持穩健，失業率仍處低檔，整體勞動市場大
致符合充分就業狀態，供需風險大致平衡；(3)通膨：官員普遍認為近期去通膨進
展有限，非住房核心服務通膨仍偏高，人力、能源及部分進口成本轉嫁情形仍值
得關注；部分官員擔憂 AI 投資與相關支出擴張，可能推升需求並增加通膨持續
性風險；(4)貨幣政策：鑑於經濟活動持續擴張、勞動市場具韌性且通膨仍高於目
標，全體官員支持升息 1 碼。許多官員認為維持較具限制性的政策立場有助降低
通膨風險，部分官員則認為未來可能仍需進一步緊縮政策，以確保通膨回到目標
水準；(5)金融風險：官員普遍認為金融條件整體仍支持經濟成長，但部分官員關
切 AI 基礎設施快速擴張所衍生的融資風險，以及美國公債市場波動、網路安全
與其他潛在金融穩定風險。 
近期美伊及中東局勢再度升溫，市場對區域衝突擴大的疑慮升高。美國總統
川普由先前宣稱期中選舉前不會攻擊伊朗，改口表示「仍有可能」採取軍事行動，
且美軍已就相關軍事選項進行準備，使美伊對抗風險再度攀升；另一方面，伊朗
雖有意透過斡旋重啟談判，但雙方在核計畫與制裁議題上仍存重大分歧。此外，
葉門青年運動持續升高對沙烏地阿拉伯攻擊，利雅德哈立德國王國際機場一週內
多次遇襲，造成多人死傷，凸顯區域安全風險升高。整體而言，若美伊衝突或代
理人戰事進一步升級，恐衝擊荷姆茲海峽及曼德海峽等能源運輸要道，推升國際
油氣價格、加劇通膨壓力，並增添全球金融市場波動及經濟成長下行風險。 
美國國際貿易委員會(USITC)於 10 月 9 日正式啟動 337 條款調查，針對特定
垂直供電系統(Vertical Power Delivery，VPD)、相關零組件及採用該技術的運算設
備是否涉及專利侵權展開審查。此案由美國電源技術廠商 Vicor Corporation 提出
申訴，主張相關產品侵犯其專利權，並要求發布有限排除令及停止侵權命令，以
限制涉案產品進入美國市場。調查對象涵蓋全球約 20 家企業，包括台灣的鴻海、
台達電、廣達及其美國關係企業，另有中國的立訊精密、工業富聯、德國的英飛
凌等國際大廠列名。 本案聚焦 AI 伺服器及高效能運算領域關鍵的供電架構技術，
該技術有助提升晶片供電效率、降低能源損耗，已廣泛應用於資料中心與 AI 運
算設備。隨著全球 AI 基礎建設投資持續擴大，相關專利布局與授權爭議亦日益
升溫。USITC 指出，此次立案僅代表調查程序正式展開，並不表示已認定侵權，
後續將由行政法官進行聽證及審理，預計於 45 天內訂定調查時程。台灣經濟部

二、歐元區—法國殖利率短期內恐難降溫 
 德國工業生產在 7 月月減 1.2%後，8 月回彈 2%，主要反映部分汽車業者在 7
月提前進行歲修，並且在 8 月陸續復工後，帶動生產及庫存回補需求所致；此外，
受到 8 月萊茵河水位降低，沿岸化學工廠物流運輸受阻，致化工產業產出月減
1.2%為主要拖累因素。儘管工業生產如期反彈，惟工業訂單卻大幅下跌 10.6%，
主要是與軍工產業相關的訂單如飛機、船舶、軍用車輛、武器彈藥等訂單減少，
顯示政府前期大額採購效應逐漸消退；除軍工產業外，其餘產業訂單則大致維持
穩定。 
 法國 8 月工業生產月減 0.3%，為連續第二個月下滑，主要係夏季用電高峰過
後電力產出回落，帶動能源部門生產減少所致。另方面，製造業生產月增 0.3%，
其中造紙、化工及電子電機設備等產業自前期低點回升，部分抵銷汽車等運輸設
備產出月減 2.2%的拖累。不過，法國 9 月 INSEE 新訂單指標由 8 月的-13.8 點降
至-15.8 點，顯示企業接單偏弱，主要反映出口需求疲軟。此外，相較於德國近期
透過國防及基礎建設支出擴大支撐工業生產，法國財政擴張力道相對有限，使製
造業景氣較依賴外部需求，德、法兩國製造業表現因而呈現逐漸分化的趨勢。 
 展望未來，近期歐元區各國殖利率上升，除反映通膨壓力增強、升息預增加
之外，亦部分反映西班牙宣布於 11 月 29 日提前進行大選、法國財政前景不明朗
等政治因素影響。儘管較高的殖利率有助於促使法國政府推動財政整頓，但考量
相關改革涉及福利及退休制度調整等政治敏感議題，不確定性較高，短期內德國
與法國殖利率差距恐難縮小。整體而言，法國製造業在出口需求疲弱及財政支撐
相對有限下，景氣復甦力道恐仍較為溫和，預料德、法兩國經濟及工業活動表現
分化的情形短期內仍將持續。 
三、日本—薪資成長支撐內需，消費信心持穩 
 8 月薪資年增 3.8%，其中經常性薪資年增 3.8%、加班費年增 5.2%、實質薪資
年增 1.5%，主要受惠於春鬥加薪效應延續及通膨壓力較去年趨緩所帶動，有助於
進一步提升民眾購買力，支撐消費與經濟復甦。

9 月消費者信心指數小幅下滑 0.1 點至 35.4，但表現仍優於市場預期。受食品
及商品價格持續上漲影響，民眾對未來一年通膨的預期升溫，預期通膨率高於 2%
的家庭比例升至 79.3%，並使生活水準及耐久財購買意願略為轉弱。不過，穩健
的薪資成長及股市上漲帶來的金融收入，持續支撐家庭所得，有助於緩衝物價上
漲對消費的負面影響，顯示日本內需仍具一定韌性。 
四、中國—歐中達成貿易談判，外部風險暫時降溫 
中國 9 月外匯存底降至 3.40 兆美元，較前月減少 381 億美元，反映美元升
值、政府以外匯購買黃金儲備，加上歐美殖利率上升，造成中國持有的海外債券
資產出現評價損失，皆為外匯存底下滑的主要原因。惟亦不排除中國外匯流入情
況可能有所轉弱，例如出口成長放緩、進口需求增加，或資本外流規模高於預期
等因素。 
展望未來，近期歐中談判達成 16 項共識，未來中國混合動力車出口至歐盟將
面臨調整壓力，但歐盟進一步升高貿易保護措施的風險已降溫。另一方面，基於
中國維持大額經常帳順差、出口商結匯需求及年底季節性資金流入支撐，預期人
民幣仍有升值空間，並帶動外匯存底回升。 
五、印度及東南亞—世銀上調經濟展望；印度央行近四年來首度升息 
(1)世界銀行 2026 年 10 月《東亞與太平洋地區經濟展望報告》指出，受人工智慧
（AI）相關出口與投資強勁帶動，將 2026 年東亞與太平洋地區（EAP）經濟成長
率預測上修 0.3 個百分點至 4.5%，2027 年與 2028 年將分別放緩至 4.4%與 4.3%，
其中，越南、馬來西亞、泰國 GDP 大幅上調至 7.4%、5.1%及 2.0%。世銀指出，
AI 相關商品占多數經濟體出口增長的一半以上，其中，馬來西亞、菲律賓、泰國
和越南的 AI 出口貢獻率更超過七成，但扣除 AI 相關產品後，區域貿易成長呈現
疲弱或負增長，警告該地區的經濟韌性高度依賴 AI 相關製造業和出口，使其容
易受到全球科技支出逆轉的影響，建議應將 AI 產品生產優勢轉化為廣泛應用，
以提升生產力，並創造更多優質就業機會。此外，美伊戰爭推升能源價格，各國
政府比其他地區更積極因應，也更仰賴補貼，此將增加財政成本並減少外匯存底，
世銀預估中東石油出口要到 2027 年中才會恢復至戰前水準，能源成本上升已對
製造業造成壓力，運輸價格大漲也已打擊消費需求，持續性的補貼可能難以為繼。

### Page 9

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

8

(2)印度:印度央行 10/7 貨幣政策會議宣布升息 1 碼至 5.5%，為近四年來首度升
息，貨幣政策立場從「中性」調整為「有節制的緊縮」。央行總裁指出，目前已
有跡象顯示，通膨預期升高，且價格壓力正逐漸擴散至更廣泛的領域。經濟展望
方面，印度內需、投資及基礎建設支出持續支撐景氣，央行將 2026/27 財年經濟
成長率預測由 6.7%上調至 7.1%，另受中東局勢緊張、國際油價上漲、食品價格
攀升及氣候因素影響，將 2026/27 財年 CPI 通膨率預測上調至 5.2%，高於 4%的
中期目標。另外，世界銀行 10 月最新《南亞經濟展望》報告，上修印度 2026/27
財年經濟成長率 0.4 個百分點至 7.1%，反映內需消費、製造業、服務業及出口表
現優於預期，然而，中東衝突導致能源價格上漲、聖嬰現象衝擊農業生產、全球
金融市場波動及資本流動風險，仍可能影響經濟前景。整體而言，印度經濟成長
動能仍穩健，但外部風險及輸入性通膨壓力增加，未來貨幣政策將以維持物價穩
定為優先，並有進一步升息的可能。 
 (3)越南: 2026 年第三季經濟成長年增率達 9.95%，高於上季修正後的 8.81%，並
創 4 年新高，累計前三季經濟成長率達 9.01%。成長動能主要來自製造業與營建
業擴張、外人直接投資持續流入、以及外貿大幅成長。各產業均穩健成長，工業
及建築業成長 12.50%(占 GDP 總值 51.57%)；服務業成長 9.54%(占 43.29%)；農
業成長 4.21%(占 5.14%)。以支出面觀之，民間消費成長 8.96%，固定投資成長
21.39%，貨物和服務出、進口分別成長 23.27%、28.75%。展望未來，越南受惠於
全球企業分散供應鏈、AI 產業需求及政府推動基礎建設與數位轉型，經濟前景仍
偏正向。

六、台灣—出口動能強勁，物價壓力與貿易風險並存 
9 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率升至 2.73%，創近兩年半新高，已連續 5 個
月高於 2%警戒線，主要係受豪雨影響，蔬果供應減少帶動價格上漲，加上汽機
車減稅效應淡化、中秋節落點不同及國際油價維持高檔，共同推升物價。除蔬果
外，外食費、房租、電費及交通相關費用皆持續上漲，顯示物價壓力仍在，但主
計總處認為整體漲勢仍屬溫和，預估 10 月 CPI 將略為回落至約 2.5%。 
9 月出口額達 872.2 億美元，年增 60.9%，其中資通產品出口倍增、電子零組
件年增 45.3%。財政部指出，在 AI 相關投資持續、部分電子產品供應偏緊、新品
上市與旺季拉貨等因素共同挹注下，出口表現明顯升溫；隨著 AI 應用擴大及年
底採購需求增加，全年出口額有望突破 9,000 億美元。 
美國國際貿易委員會（USITC）於 10 月 9 日依據《1930 年關稅法》第 337 條
款，對特定垂直供電系統及相關產品啟動專利侵權調查，其中台達電、廣達及鴻
海旗下相關企業等遭列名。若 337 條款調查最終認定侵權，相關產品可能遭禁止
輸入美國，後續應關注調查結果及對台灣電源供應與伺服器供應鏈的影響。

⚫ 長期佈局機器人與 CPO 商機 
機器人業務佔 26Q3 營收達 12%，其中 EFEM 隨半導體需
求熱絡穩健成長，並已超前部署對應 FOPLP 方片規格之移載
系統。公司充分發揮垂直整合優勢，自製減速機、螺桿、軸承，
並整合關係企業大銀微系統之伺服馬達與驅動器。目前已獲美
系人形機器人廠商小批量線性滑軌訂單，美系 AI 物流機器人
放量時程則取決於客戶端實測進度。CPO 方面，大銀微已開
發出奈米級定位平台，並整合上銀微小型螺桿與線軌，共同搶
攻次世代半導體設備商機。

More details on SBC — SBC's 9M26 sales declined by a single digit y/y with stable 
GPM and improving opex. Pre-tax profits rose 40% y/y in 9M26. Yet the higher 
effective tax rate dragged profitability, leading to a  net loss of US$25mn in 9M26. 
Mgmt expects SBC to launch more new products in 2027E which should further 
drive the top-line and earnings recovery.  
Implications — We expect a negative share price reaction to the results print given 
the weaker-than-expected core biz and the SBC loss. Mgmt expects FY26 sales to 
fall 14-15% y/y with 4Q OEM sales seasonally softer than 3Q. New models are due 
from Jan-27 and mgmt expects low-teens growth y/y for FY27E. We will look for 
more detail on the drivers of the expected sales recovery at its 3Q26 results briefing 
next Tuesday, Nov 17.

### Page 3

Merida (9914.TW)

3
Merida
Valuation
Our target price for Merida shares of NT$93 is based on a 2H26-1H27E P/E of 15.5x, a 30% discount to its historical average of
22x during pre-Covid years (2014-2019), in view of soft order momentum as a result of slow recovery in bike demand, a volatile
margin trend, and low visibility of SBC.
Risks
Downside risks that could mean the Merida shares trade below our target price include: 1) e-bike adoption slower than

expected; 2) weaker restocking demand; 3) lukewarm sell-through at SBC; and 4) a weaker macro.
Upside risks that could mean the Merida shares trade above our target price include: 1) e-bike adoption faster than expected;
2) stronger restocking demand; 3) stronger sell-through at SBC; and 4) a strong macro.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

buy recommendation and a Catalyst Watch/STV Downside call corresponds to a sell recommendation. Any stock not assigned

### Page 6

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

MPI Corp. (6223.TWO): Richer MEMS mix to drive GM expansion ahead; 
3Q26 beat; TP up to NT$8,600; Buy
12 November 2026 | 12:27AM HKT
Oct revenues on track: Oct revenues came in at NT$2,232mn, +93% YoY and +9% 
MoM (vs. 3Q26 revenues +13% QoQ), accounting for 33% of our 4Q26E revenue 
forecast. Looking into 4Q26, we expect revenue to grow 13% QoQ on continued 
AI-driven demand for both VPC and MEMS probe cards and initial shipments to the 
US CPU customer, and we raise our GM assumption to 62.4% (vs. 60.4% previously) 
to reflect the richer VPC/MEMS mix seen in 3Q26. For 2027, we expect MEMS 
capacity additions, share gains at the US CPU customer and rising pin count per card 
to drive growth, with GM expanding further as MEMS mix rises. 3Q26 beat: Revenue 
was pre-announced. GM of 61.7% came in above GSe/Bloomberg consensus of 
59.8%/59.5%, which we attribute to higher VPC/MEMS shipments for AI-related 
applications. Together with a lower opex ratio of 23.1% (vs. GSe/cons. of 
23.6%/24.1%) on larger revenue scale, operating income grew 26% QoQ, 8.5% / 
11.5% ahead of GSe/cons. EPS of NT$19.18 was 8.1% / 11.0% above GSe/cons. With 
higher GM, we raise our 2026-28E EPS by 5%/3%/1%, and raise our 12m TP to 
NT$8,600 (implied 53x 2027E P/E vs. 90% NI YoY of 2027-28E on avg.). Maintain Buy.

MEMS capacity ramp and new customers to drive growth: Our 4Q26E forecast 
implies average monthly revenue of NT$2,226mn in November-December (flat vs. 
October). On the margin front, we expect product mix to remain favorable for GM, as 
VPC/MEMS capacity additions and new customer ramps continue. Longer term, we 
remain positive on MPI’s growth outlook, driven by 1) MEMS and VPC capacity 
expansion, where we keep our capacity assumption of 2.5mn/8.0mn pins per month 
for VPC/MEMS by end-2027E, which we see as well supported as multiple AI ASIC

and networking programs move into volume; 2) share gains at the US CPU customer,

Earnings revisions: We incorporate the 3Q26 results into our model and revise our 
2026/2027/2028E EPS by +4.8%/+3.2%/+1.2%, as we factor in 1) higher GM, reflecting 
the 3Q26 results and a richer VPC/MEMS mix, and 2) higher revenue 
(+0.8%/+1.2%/+1.5% vs. our prior forecasts). Overall, we now model revenue growth of 
63%/106%/47% YoY over 2026-28E (vs. 61%/105%/46% previously), with GM 
expanding to 60.7%/63.1%/63.9% (vs. 59.6%/62.2%/63.7% previously).

Valuation: As a result of our earnings revisions, we raise our 12-month TP to NT$8,600 
(from NT$8,500). Our 12-month TP is still based on a target P/E of 40x (1.7x of 3-year 
average forward P/E) applied to our 2028E EPS, and discounted back to 2027E at 12.4% 
CoE. Our key assumptions for CoE include: (1) 1.3x beta (sourcing Bloomberg), (2) a 
4.25% risk-free rate, and (3) a market risk premium at 6.25% (in line with GS house view).

Price Target Risks and Methodology – MPI (6223.TWO) 
Valuation: We are Buy rated on MPI. Our 12-month TP of NT$8,600 is based on a target 
P/E of 40x (1.7x of 3-year average forward P/E) applied to our 2028E EPS, and 
discounted back to 2027E at 12.4% CoE. Our key assumptions for CoE include: (1) 1.3x 
beta (sourcing Bloomberg), (2) a 4.25% risk-free rate, and (3) a market risk premium at 
6.25% (in line with GS house view). 
Key risks to our views: 1) softer AI/HPC demand, 2) slower penetration into new TAM, 
and 3) intensifying competition.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

CPO 設備將於 3Q27 量產，給 Buy 的投資建議。

獲利大幅成長，CPO 設備將於 3Q27 量產，給 Buy 的投資建議。

PCB 背鑽計量領域的地位依然穩固；大量受惠於 AI 伺服器帶
動高階 PCB 鑽孔設備需求的趨勢，而非單純受惠某一客戶的拉
貨。

元。

CCD 機種及 TM3、 TM4。一般機種毛利率約 20~30%，高階機
種毛利率約 50%， TM3、 TM4 毛利率更高，TM4 於 3Q26 出貨
約 5 台。

先進智能上櫃後，大量持股大量先進智能仍會超過 60%。

(Metrology)、 AOI 智慧檢測(Inspection)及 (Automation)自動化。
產品應用包含 CoWoS、 SoIC 及面板級封裝(FOPLP)(CoPoS)、
CPO 等。

2 群益投顧 
  ESG：  
E：(Environmental)。大量科技秉持「愛護地球、永續發展」之理念，持續推
動環境管理制度，並透過日常營運管理落實環境保護與污染預防，降低營運
活動對環境造成之影響。大量科技秉持「愛護地球、永續發展」之理念，持
續推動環境管理制度，並透過日常營運管理落實環境保護與污染預防，降低
營運活動對環境造成之影響。大量科技針對廠區內原物料、製程、生產活動
及服務建立環境考量評估機制，評估範圍涵蓋正常、異常及緊急狀況下可能
產生之環境影響，並納入空氣排放、水污染、廢棄物、能源使用、土地污染、
噪音及其他環境衝擊等項目。 
S：(Social)。大量科技重視人才發展與員工照顧，將員工視為公司穩健營運
與永續經營的重要基礎。大量科技秉持以人為本的精神，致力於培育與留任
專業人才，並透過多元招募、薪酬福利、教育訓練、勞資溝通及權益保障等
制度，持續打造友善、平等且具支持性的職場環境。大量科技於人才任用上
遵循相關勞動法令及多元平等原則，重視員工專業能力發展及工作權益保
障，並持續透過教育訓練提升員工技能，強化員工與公司共同成長之基礎。
G：(Governance)。大量科技致力於推動產品朝高規格、高自動化、高智能化
及完全整合化之方向發展，以貼近客戶需求，並與客戶共同開發新產品。此
外，公司持續投入自動化設備之研發，厚植核心技術能力，並持續開發進階
產品，以掌握市場先機。為確保制度有效運作，大量科技於企劃室下設企業
誠信經營小組，由總經理擔任召集人，負責誠信經營政策之推動與執行情形
之監督，並每年向董事會報告執行成果及防範不誠信行為之措施。大量科技
依董事會通過之「誠信經營守則」及相關內部規章，建立對於不誠信行為之
相關防範機制，並透過內部控制、檢舉制度及教育訓練等措施，確保誠信經
營政策之落實。 
公司簡介： 
大量的產主要產品應用於製造、半導體先進製程與先進封裝，以及光電、玻
璃基板與精密材料加工等領域。透過自主研發之控制器、應用軟體、視覺定
位、深度控制、運動控制及量測檢測技術，大量科技依客戶製程需求提供設
備及技術服務。 
PCB 設備：PCB 鑽孔定位、PCB 成型切割、邊緣滾塗、針塗等塗佈製程及
高速型內層銅深度量測機等。 
半導體設備：CMP Pad 即時量測監控設備、3D IC 先進封裝製程晶圓及晶片
量測設備、FOPLP 形貌量測設備、3DIC 先進封裝製程檢測設備、Flip Chip 
製程檢測設備、捲帶包裝晶片本體及外觀缺陷之檢查設備等、封裝製程自動
化設備等。 
大量 PCB 客戶的客戶包含台灣、中國的 PCB 廠；半導體設備客戶包含晶圓
代工廠、OSAT。  
高階背鑽設備的市場，主要同業為德國 Schmoll Maschinen。  
資料來源：大量年報。

大量的子公司-大量先進智能規劃 IPO：  
大量先進智能已經於 08/01/2026 獨立，目前大量持股大量先進智能 100%，
預計大量先進智能未來上櫃後，大量持股大量先進智能仍會超過 60%。  
大量先進智能的核心技術包含了半導體的精密量測(Metrology)、AOI 智慧檢
測 (Inspection)及 (Automation)自動化。產品應用包含 CoWoS、 SoIC 及面板
級封裝(FOPLP)(CoPoS)、 CPO 等。

資料來源：大量法說簡報。 
2027 年 PCB 與半導體設備展望皆正向： 
PCB 設備 2027 年每月都有要交機，交期約 5~6 月；半導體設備 B/B ratio 維
持在 2 以上。 
大量預估 2027 年 CCD 成型機與鑽孔機將維持雙位數成長，TM3 完成階段
性任務後出貨將減少，TM4 出貨量預期會有大幅倍數成長。PCB 在手訂單
有達千台。

5 群益投顧 
大量有 CPO 相關設備，單價較一般半導體設備高： 
大量目前與北美 Tier 1 客戶合作，2026 年預計完成第一條產線的建置，量
產時程預計落在 3Q27。 2027 年設備需求量將倍數成長。 
由於該設備為整合光學前處理、檢測、後處理的一體機，結合了 3~4 項關鍵
技術，平均售價(ASP)約為一般晶圓檢測設備的 300~400%。

8 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

Company Brief 
유틸리티/건설 
Analyst 문경원, CFA 
kyeongwon.moon@meritz.co.kr 
RA 이동석 
dongseok.lee@meritz.co.kr 3Q26 Review: 바닥을 잡는다면 지금  
믹스 개선에 따른 이익률 개선 조금씩 관찰 중 
3Q26 연결 영업이익은 2,612억원(+90.6% YoY)을 기록하며 컨센서스(2,455억원)
를 소폭 상회했다. 밥캣(1,674억원)이 컨센서스(1,761억원)를 소폭 하회한 가운데, 
에너빌리티 부문 영업이익(+179% YoY, OPM 6.6%)이 호실적을 이끌었다. 환율 하
락에도 불구, 가스터빈, 원전 등 고수익성 프로젝트의 비중 상승이 주요 원인이다. 
3Q26 누적 수주는 10.0조원(+85.5% YoY)을 기록했다. 4Q26 가스복합 EPC 수주 
등을 감안하면 연간 가이던스(13.3조원) 달성은 무난하다. 한수원과의 협상 결렬로 
이목이 집중된 체코 두코바니 시공 수주는 2027년 인식될 확률이 높아 보인다. 주간
사가 바뀌더라도 이미 프로젝트에 참여 중인 동사를 배제하기는 쉽지 않다.

대미투자 이후, 연말~연초 수주 모멘텀에 집중 
연말 연초에 다음과 수주 모멘텀이 집중되면서, 주가 상승을 이끌 전망이다. 
1) 대형원전: 정부 자금 중심의 Westinghouse LLI(장납기 기자재) 발주가 몰린다. 
우선 미국 DOE(에너지부)가 제공한 대출을 기반으로 미국 수주가 기대된다.  폴란드 
정부 역시 최근 LLI 발주 작업을 시작했다. 한편, 한국 정부는 연말까지 100억 달러 
규모의 대미 투자 송금을 협의 중인데, 이 역시 DOC(상무부) 주도의 대형 원전 건설
을 위한 LLI 발주에 쓰일 가능성이 높다 
2) SMR: 오래 기다려왔던 Nuscale 향 수주가 시작되고 있다. 우선 29일 모듈 1기 
제작 계약을 체결했다. 현금이 부족한 Nuscale이 용기를 냈다는 건, 곧 Nuscale-
TVA 간 PPA 가 임박했음을 의미한다. 본격적인 수주는 PPA 직후 예상된다.  
3) 가스 터빈: 2026년 초 해외 수주(7기)는 2029년까지 인도 계획이다. 이후 2030
년 슬랏 예약을 완료하고 2031년 물량을 논의 중으로 보인다. 지금은 2030년 슬랏
에 대한 PO가 나와 수주잔고에 인식할 타이밍이다. 역시 수출 물량으로 추측한다.

성장에 대한 기대가 사라진 밸류에이션, 지금이 바닥 
투자의견 Buy를 유지, 할인율 상승을 반영하여 적정주가를 109,000원으로 -17% 
하향 조정한다. 금리 상승, 환율 하락, 트럼프 지지율 하락 등 Top-down 리스크에 
최근 조정을 받았지만, 추가적인 주가 하락 폭은 제한적일 전망이다. EV/선행 수주잔
고 지표는 현재 1.0배 수준까지 하락하며, 원전의 성장성이 주목받지 못했던 
2021~2024년 수준에 근접하게 내려왔다.  
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 30

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

Document 284: AI抛售潮里，SpaceX凭什么扛住了？

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI抛售潮里，SpaceX凭什么扛住了？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

⼤摩认为， SpaceX 正在与传统的 “AI 交易 ” 脱钩，背后有三⼤⽀撑：星链 " 类债券 " 属性（⼀
颗星舰卫星回收期仅 20 周）、 Terafab ⾄ 2035 年⾼达 4900 亿美元半导体供应链资本开⽀、
以及估值极度低估 ——2028 年预期市盈率仅 11.2 倍，远低于标普 500 的 16 倍。
摘要：
在近期剧烈波动的 AI 抛售潮中， SpaceX 展现出了惊⼈的抗跌韧性。摩根⼠丹利认为， SpaceX
正在与传统的 “AI 交易 ” 脱钩（ Decorrelate ），成为投资者投资组合中更具持久性的避⻛港，维持
其 “ 增持 ” 评级，⽬标价⾼达 300 美元。
据追⻛交易台消息，摩根⼠丹利在 11 ⽉ 17 ⽇的研报中称， SpaceX 的核⼼吸引⼒在于三⼤催化
剂：星链（ Starlink ）业务展现出的 “ 类债券 ” 稳定收益特征、旗下 Terafab 在重塑美国半导体供
应链中的巨额资本开⽀计划（⾄ 2035 年达 4900 亿美元），以及当前极具吸引⼒的估值⽔平

（ 2028 年预期市盈率远低于标普 500 指数）。
不过，报告还指出， SpaceX 与特斯拉未来关系的悬⽽未决，仍是⽬前压制股价的最⼤不确定性
因素。
核⼼业务的 “ 类债券 ” 属性：星链（ Starlink ）的惊⼈回报率
随着 SpaceX 在零售、企业和政府市场的连接业务（星链）收⼊重新加速，投资者将重新认识到
这项核⼼业务独特的“ 类债券 ” 属性。
⼤摩测算数据显⽰，发射满载 V3 宽带卫星的星舰（ Starship ）（总容量 50 TBs ，可售容量 2.5
TBs ），按 2030 年预估运⾏率计算，可产⽣约 3 亿⾄ 4 亿美元的收⼊，⽽其增量息税前利润
（ EBIT ）的投资回收期仅约为 20 周。
此外，来⾃即将进⾏的测试以及⾮ SpaceX 实体加速太空 AI 投资，将进⼀步验证天基 AI 基础设施
的价值。
押注 “Tera'Merica” ：重塑美国半导体供应链的巨额资本开⽀
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

在美国对尖端 AI 半导体技术外流审查⽇益严格的背景下，美国实现晶圆⽣产⾃给⾃⾜和半导体供
应链独⽴的必要性空前⾼涨。 SpaceX/Terafab 在这⼀进程中扮演着潜在的重⼤⻆⾊。
⼤摩在 SpaceX 的模型中，为 Terafab 分配了⾄ 2030 年约 1700 亿美元的资本开⽀，⾄ 2035 年的资
本开⽀更是⾼达 4900 亿美元，其中明年的资本开⽀就将达到 150 亿美元。
最⼤的悬念： SpaceX 与特斯拉的未来关系
尽管基本⾯强劲，但⽬前阻碍最乐观的投资者建仓或加仓的最⼤压制因素，是 SpaceX 与特斯拉
未来关系的不确定性（包括两家公司董事会独⽴委员会的最终意⻅）。
⼤摩接触的⼤多数投资者虽然普遍认可潜在合并的价值，但对合并时机缺乏确定性以及潜在的
股权稀释⻛险感到担忧。
估值极具吸引⼒：远低于标普 500 的预期市盈率
⽬前 SpaceX 的交易价格约为 140 美元 / 股，仅⽐其 135 美元 / 股的 IPO 发⾏价⾼出 4%。⽽⾃今年 7
⽉初以来，⼤摩对 SpaceX 2027 年和 2028 年的每股收益（ EPS ）预期已分别⼤幅上调了 80% 和

60%。
剔除任何税收优惠， SpaceX ⽬前的交易价格是⼤摩 2028 年盈利预期的 17.5 倍。这⼀预期相
对保守，仅假设 2028 年底达到 8.3 GW （相⽐之下，⻢斯克指引的 2027 年底为 13 GW ， 2028
年底预估为 14 GW ），且每⽡收⼊从 2027 年第⼀季度的 34 美元 / ⽡降⾄ 2028 财年的 21 美元 /
⽡。
若采⽤ Visible Alpha 的共识预期（剔除税收优惠），SpaceX 2028 年的预期市盈率（ P/E ）仅
为 11.2 倍。作为对⽐，标普 500 指数⽬前的 2028 年预期市盈率为 16 倍，凸显了 SpaceX 相对低
廉的估值。
⼤摩给出的300 美元⽬标价基于分部加总（ SOTP ）估值法，具体拆解为：太空业务（ Space ） 8
美元，连接业务（ Connectivity ） 111 美元， X & Grok 11 美元，企业 AI （ Enterprise AI ） 170
⽬前的焦点在于这些巨额资⾦的具体去向，以及将为 SpaceX 股东带来怎样的投资回报。

Document 295: Asia Outlook 2027_ Growth broadens, capital gets selective

Source PDF: all report pdf/Asia Outlook 2027_ Growth broadens, capital gets selective.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 25
Status: ok

### Page 1

After a stronger-than-expected 2026, Asian growth should
broaden in 2027 as AI tailwinds moderate, lower energy
prices support demand and supply-chain diversiﬁcation
continues. Capital ﬂows will become more selective as global
ﬁnancial conditions tighten. We favor the KRW, TWD, SGD, and
expect Australia and Philippines to lead easing moves
Deepali Bhargava
How did Asia’s economy perform in 2026,
and what will look different in 2027?
2026 was stronger than expected

Asia surprised on the upside in 2026, with regional GDP growth
outperforming expectations, led by strong exports and investment
activity. Investment remained robust across much of the region,
supported by technology-related spending and government-led
infrastructure projects. This enabled export-oriented economies
such as South Korea, Taiwan and Singapore to signiﬁcantly exceed
consensus growth forecasts.

### Page 2

The picture was less favourable in economies more reliant on
domestic demand. Indonesia and the Philippines disappointed
expectations as household consumption remained subdued.
Higher energy prices weighed on real incomes, while the Philippines
also faced a drag from slower government spending amid political
and corruption-related controversies. Both economies attracted
relatively less tech-related foreign direct investment than the
region's manufacturing and semiconductor hubs, limiting support

from global dynamics.
India was a notable outperformer. Despite beneﬁting less directly
from the AI-driven boom and facing higher energy import costs, it
expanded by a robust 8.2% year-on-year in the ﬁrst half of 2026.
Growth continued to be supported by structural reforms, public
capital expenditure and subsidies that cushioned households from
higher fuel prices.
AI-led divergences should narrow in 2027, favouring South Asia
Overall, we expect GDP growth excluding China to slow to 3.8% in

2027, down from 4.5% in 2026. Tight chip supply should continue
to support investment growth through 2027, but as capex growth
slows from today's exceptional pace, AI's contribution to growth is
likely to moderate.
As AI tailwinds fade and the full eﬀects of monetary tightening are
felt, growth in developed Asia, particularly Korea and Australia,
could slow more than in developing Asia. Korea is the clearest
example, where growth has been concentrated in technology-

related sectors while domestic demand, non-tech industries and
broader wage growth have lagged. As in previous investment-led
booms, the beneﬁts have been slower to ﬁlter through to the wider
economy.
By contrast, the Philippines’ 2026 growth disappointment was
driven primarily by policy and political uncertainty, both of which
are likely to prove temporary. India continues to occupy a relative
sweet spot. While it beneﬁted less from the AI boom, it’s also less

exposed to any correction. Structural reforms, infrastructure

### Page 3

spending, and resilient services exports should leave it relatively
well placed as the AI cycle normalises.
The divergence is less pronounced in parts of Southeast Asia,
particularly Singapore and Malaysia, where domestic demand has
remained relatively resilient alongside a strong external sector.
Trade growth likely to moderate in 2027
Asian economies are expected to be the largest contributors to
global trade volume growth for the third consecutive year in 2026.

According to World Trade Organisation forecasts, global trade
growth is expected to moderate in 2027 as AI-related demand
cools from exceptionally strong levels, though the deceleration
should be gradual rather than abrupt.
Recent data has been mixed. Some signs of moderation appear in
semiconductor export growth in Japan and Taiwan, and export
orders softened across the board in October. However,
semiconductor exports from Korea and China continue to post

strong gains. Data from Taiwan suggest it’s still too early to call a
turning point.
Even so, trade growth should remain relatively resilient in 2027,
supported by ongoing supply-chain diversiﬁcation, continued
investment ﬂows and still-elevated levels of AI-related activity. This
is even as the pace of expansion normalises.
Pockets of domestic demand strength should support growth
The structural backdrop for consumption in Japan is becoming
more supportive. Wage growth has increased signiﬁcantly since the

start of the year. Also, households are gradually shifting away from
the cash-heavy behaviour associated with the deﬂation era
towards greater participation in ﬁnancial markets. This supports
wealth creation and consumption.
Singapore should continue to see resilient consumption growth,
supported by strong FDI inﬂows and spillovers from the AI
investment cycle. These tailwinds are feeding into ﬁrm wage
growth and stronger household purchasing power. Singapore has

In India, consumer spending was relatively insulated from the

How is AI reshaping Asia's growth story,
and can the region continue to beneﬁt as
the boom matures?
Looking ahead to 2027, North Asia should continue to dominate AI
trade. Southeast Asia should play an increasingly important role as
its AI story is driven by production, investment and supply-chain

integration rather than AI adoption itself. While the region doesn’t
have the largest AI companies, it’s become indispensable to
hardware and manufacturing ecosystems that make AI possible.
As ﬁrms diversify supply chains and invest in AI infrastructure,
Southeast Asia stands out as one of the largest structural
beneﬁciaries of the AI boom outside North Asia.

### Page 5

Trade dominance: Asia accounted for about 77% of AI-driven
exports in 2025
Asia sits at the centre of the global AI hardware ecosystem. It
dominates global trade in AI-enabling goods. East Asia, excluding
China, accounts for more than 40% of global exports of AI-related
products. Southeast Asia contributes another quarter despite
having a much smaller share of overall world trade. With China
contributing another 13%, Asia is generating much of the world's

growth in AI-related exports.
The same dynamic is visible on the import side. AI-enabling goods
account for a far larger share of imports than East Asia’s and
Southeast Asia’s share of overall trade, underscoring the deep
integration of global AI supply chains.
Growth in the ﬁrst half of 2026 reinforced Asia's leadership, with the
region's largest AI exporters recording the fastest growth. In the
second quarter, exports of AI-enabling goods surged by 80% YoY.

East Asia’s contribution to global export growth doubled to 32.3
percentage points in just six months, while Southeast Asia’s share
similarly surged to 22.0 percentage points. China alone accounted
for a further 12.8 percentage points, underscoring the region’s
outsized role in driving global trade.
These trade relationships have shifted markedly over the last
decade. One notable change is Southeast Asia's growing share of
US imports, consistent with the rapid build-out of data centres by

hyperscaler ﬁrms. Yet Southeast Asia has lagged in strengthening
trade linkages with East Asia, excluding China, and within the
region itself. This suggests there’s scope for deeper regional
integration as the AI ecosystem expands.
Broadening beneﬁciaries: Asia’s AI story extends beyond
Korea/Taiwan
On products, much of the focus has been on memory chip exports
from Korea and Taiwan. But the beneﬁciaries of the AI boom are
increasingly broadening. ASEAN economies have attracted

substantial foreign direct investment in semiconductors ucand

### Page 6

increased their share of global non-memory chip production.
Singapore and Malaysia now account for more than 33% of global
ampliﬁer chip exports, up from 23% a decade ago. The Philippines
and Thailand have made impressive gains, together accounting for
around 2.5% of global exports.
Looking across the AI value chain, almost all AI-enabling goods
trade is concentrated in intermediate inputs (62%) and equipment
(37%). While East Asia, ex-China, remains the dominant supplier at

every stage of the value chain, Southeast Asia has emerged as a
critical second hub. It accounts for roughly a quarter of global
exports of AI-related equipment, spanning the full value chain from
semiconductor manufacturing to the servers and other
infrastructure deployed in data centres. This is particularly
signiﬁcant because the fastest-growing areas of AI investment are
in physical infrastructure, including data centres, servers, and
semiconductor packaging and testing, where Southeast Asia has

built a strong competitive advantage.
Southeast Asia's importance is especially evident in the middle of
the value chain. Together, East Asia and Southeast Asia account for
nearly 90% of global trade in AI-related intermediate inputs, with
Southeast Asia contributing around 23%.
While growth in AI-related demand may eventually moderate,
Asia's central role in hardware and manufacturing ecosystems
should help sustain investment, trade and industrial activity. This

should keep the region a key beneﬁciary of the AI cycle for years to
come.

### Page 7

Note: AI enabling goods refer to products that facilitate the use of AI in
various sectors, and are part of a list used in 2025 World Trade Report.
Source: WTO
Is AI driving inﬂation or productivity in
Asia? And is it disrupting jobs or
transforming them?
AI's economic impact in Asia is likely to be uneven over time. Signs
of AI-related inﬂation are already visible, particularly in Korea,
where technology-related price increases are contributing
meaningfully to headline CPI. By contrast, evidence of productivity

improvements remains concentrated in parts of the technology
sector and highly AI-complementary occupations. While the
productivity beneﬁts could ultimately prove more signiﬁcant,
they’re likely to emerge gradually and take longer to be reﬂected in
economy-wide data.
AI is starting to show up in Asia's inﬂation data, especially Korea
AI is likely to be inﬂationary in the short term. This is reﬂected in the
sharp rise in memory chip prices and strong tech exports, as

demand for chips continues to outpace supply. Recent consumer
price data suggest electronics prices are beginning to recover
across parts of Asia, with the strongest increases seen in mobile
phones, computers and communication equipment.
The sharpest increases have been in Korea, where data storage
device prices rose around 40% YoY year-to-date. Inﬂation is
accelerating rapidly in these categories. Mobile phone prices rose
11% YoY in October, up from just 2% at the start of the year.

Computer-related prices accelerated to 24% YoY from 6% earlier in

### Page 8

the year. Mobile handset and communication device inﬂation has
also picked up in India and Indonesia, reaching 6-8% YoY.
Contribution to headline inﬂation varies across Asia, but generally
remains low given the lower weights of technology-related
components. Korea is seeing one of the region's largest increases,
with AI-related categories adding about 20bp to headline inﬂation.
In most other Asian economies, the contribution remains below 10
basis points despite price increases, reﬂecting much smaller CPI

weights.
Evidence of AI-related inﬂation in services is less clear-cut.
Information and communication services inﬂation has picked up in
Korea and Malaysia, while falling communication charges continue
to weigh on technology-related CPI components in Singapore.
Overall, however, the contribution to headline inﬂation remains
modest.
Emerging productivity gains
On productivity, the distinction between AI producers and AI users
matters. The IMF ﬁnds that advanced Asian economies like

Singapore, Korea and Japan have a relatively high concentration of
jobs where AI is more likely to augment workers than replace them,
creating greater potential for productivity gains. Around 40% of
jobs in Singapore are estimated to be highly complementary to AI.
The key question, however, is whether these gains are already
visible in the data. Direct evidence remains limited given the short
time frame of AI adoption. Australia is one of the few economies in

the region where available data draw some tentative conclusions.
Our analysis of changes in real output per worker suggests
productivity growth has improved in parts of the technology sector,
particularly telecommunications services. While it’s still too early to
attribute this entirely to AI, the evidence is consistent with
technology adoption beginning to support productivity growth in
selected industries.
Early evidence points to adaptation, not job losses

### Page 9

Evidence from Korea suggests AI may be reshaping labour demand
unevenly. Youth employment has weakened in highly AI-exposed
industries, while employment among workers in their 50s has
increased. India and the Philippines are among the countries most
exposed to AI because of their sizeable outsourcing sectors. Early
evidence points to job transformation and augmentation rather
than widespread displacement.
In the Philippines, employment in information and

communications, professional services, and scientiﬁc and technical
services has grown by around 4.5% over the past year. Recent
readings remain close to long-term trends. Digitally delivered
services exports continue to expand, even as the composition
shifts. Financial services and computer services are increasingly
driving growth, while the share of more traditional business process
outsourcing activities becomes less dominant.
The evidence from India points in a similar direction. Rather than

widespread layoﬀs, AI's impact appears to be showing up through
slower hiring and changing skill requirements. While AI is reducing
demand for some traditional roles, it’s creating demand for new
skills. Traditional IT ﬁrms are hiring more cautiously, but global
capability centres (GCCs) continue to expand and absorb higher-
skilled talent. As a result, India's business services exports have
more than doubled as a share of GDP since the pandemic. Even as

AI automates more routine coding and back-oﬃce tasks, demand
remains strong for higher-value activities such as software
development, analytics, AI integration and cybersecurity.
AI is already contributing to higher inﬂation
in Korea

How are Asia's trade and investment
relationships shifting?
Diversiﬁcation, not deglobalisation
The key lesson from 2026 is that tariﬀs and geopolitical tensions
are reshaping trade patterns rather than materially reducing
volumes. Rather than exiting Asia, ﬁrms have responded by
diversifying production, sourcing, and investment across a wider
range of regional economies.

Tariﬀs remain an important risk, particularly if the Trump
administration refocuses on trade policy. Recent experience
suggests higher tariﬀs are more likely to shift where production
occurs than to reverse the broader diversiﬁcation trend. While
economies such as Vietnam and Thailand may face greater
scrutiny after gaining signiﬁcant US market share, tariﬀ
diﬀerentials with China are likely to remain large enough to keep
incentives for ﬁrms to maintain diversiﬁed regional supply chains.

### Page 11

Diversiﬁcation does not mean decoupling from China
Despite tariﬀs, Asia ex-China's trade surplus with the US has almost
doubled since the end of 2021, driven primarily by Taiwan, Vietnam
and Thailand. Over the same period, much of the rise in ASEAN’s
trade surplus with the US was mirrored by a corresponding decline
in China’s surplus, pointing to a reconﬁguration rather than a
reduction of regional trade ﬂows. The pattern suggests supply
chains are being reconﬁgured across Asia rather than shifting away

from the region altogether.
More importantly, production relocation has not meaningfully
reduced China's role in regional supply chains. While ﬁnal assembly
and exports to the US increasingly take place in economies such as
Vietnam and Thailand, a signiﬁcant share of intermediate inputs
still originates in China. This means supply chains are becoming
more geographically diversiﬁed but remain closely integrated with
China.
Europe is becoming a more important trade and investment

partner
Asia's trade and investment relationships are becoming more
diversiﬁed, with Europe playing an increasingly important role.
Trade ties have strengthened signiﬁcantly in recent years, and Asia
continues to gain export market share in Europe. Since 2024,
ASEAN exports to the EU have risen by around 30%. ASEAN now
exports more to Europe than Korea, Taiwan and Japan combined.
Looking ahead, a renewed push towards free-trade agreements
could provide further support. The proposed India-EU Free Trade

Agreement is particularly signiﬁcant, while a growing network of
agreements between ASEAN economies and European partners is
helping to lower barriers to trade and investment.
The investment relationship with Europe is strengthening even
faster. EU foreign direct investment into ASEAN surged to $31 billion
in 2021, overtaking the US as ASEAN's second-largest source of FDI.
More importantly, investment decisions are increasingly shaped by
strategic imperatives rather than cost competitiveness alone.

European ﬁrms increasingly view ASEAN not just as a

manufacturing base, but as a strategic hub for regional operations,
supply-chain diversiﬁcation, and long-term growth. Reﬂecting this

and storage.

Has inﬂation peaked in Asia, and what
are the key risks to 2027?
Inﬂation outlook 2027: Lower headline gains but stickier core

pressures
Though it’s too early to call a peak in inﬂation in most of Asia, we
do expect prices to moderate in many parts of the region in 2027,
driven by easing energy and food prices. The largest declines
should occur in economies such as the Philippines and Singapore,
where recent inﬂation was driven primarily by fuel and food shocks.
Economies at the centre of the AI hardware ecosystem, particularly
Korea and Taiwan, may experience stickier core inﬂation as

technology-related price pressures continue to build.
While headline inﬂation should move lower, the balance of risks is
shifting from commodity-driven inﬂation towards technology-
driven inﬂation. This implies that while goods inﬂation may
moderate as energy and food pressures fade, core inﬂation could
remain stickier than expected. As a result, inﬂation may prove more

### Page 13

persistent than historical relationships with energy and food prices
alone would suggest.
Energy and food disinﬂation should drive the decline in inﬂation
Inﬂation has likely not yet peaked across most Asian economies,
but we expect it to do so in 4Q26-1Q27, with a clearer moderation
emerging from 2Q27. The key driver is energy prices.
We expect Brent crude to average around US$77/bbl in 2027,
around 14% below 2026 levels. While occasional US-Iran tensions

should keep some geopolitical risk premium in the market, we
expect a growing supply surplus to outweigh these concerns
provided there are no major disruptions to Persian Gulf exports. A
US-Iran agreement would present further downside risk to oil prices
and, by extension, inﬂation across Asia. Reﬁned fuel prices should
moderate year on year. Diesel prices could decline more sharply as
crack spreads normalise following the return of Russian supply,

although margins are likely to remain above historical averages.
Inﬂation should ease more noticeably from the second quarter of
2027, aided by favorable energy base eﬀects as oil prices were
unusually high a year earlier. Lower oil prices should also gradually
ﬁlter through to retail fuel costs, helping push headline inﬂation
lower across much of developing Asia.
The disinﬂationary impact should be most pronounced in oil-
importing economies such as the Philippines and Singapore, where

fuel has been a major driver of recent inﬂation pressures. In
Singapore, fuel prices accounted for almost 90% of the YoY increase
in headline CPI since February 2026. In Australia, transport costs
accounted for more than 100% of the increase in headline inﬂation,
oﬀset by weakness elsewhere in the CPI basket. In the Philippines,
fuel accounted for roughly 60% of the direct increase in inﬂation.
The overall impact is likely larger once second-round eﬀects

through transport, services and other fuel-sensitive categories are
taken into account. As lower oil prices feed through, these
economies should experience some of the largest declines in
inﬂation across the region.

Food should provide a second source of disinﬂation in 2027. As El
Niño fades in the second half, weather-related pressures on
agricultural production should ease. More broadly, demand and
supply conditions in key food markets appear more balanced than
in recent years, supporting a gradual moderation in food price
inﬂation.
The key wildcard is AI-related inﬂation. If these price increases
persist, second-round eﬀects could become more important
through services costs and wages in technology-intensive sectors.

Lower oil prices should help ease inﬂation,
while core pressures could persist
Source: CEIC
Are food price risks from EL Niño behind
us?
Probably not. The current El Niño is expected to remain strong to
very strong through 1Q27, and there remains a meaningful risk that
weather-related food price pressures persist into next year.
That said, a severe El Niño does not automatically translate into
higher inﬂation. Historical experience is mixed. During both the

2015-16 and 2023-24 El Niño episodes, headline inﬂation across
most of Asia was little changed, with only limited evidence of a
broad-based inﬂation shock. Taiwan was a notable exception in
2015-16. A key reason was that other commodity prices,
particularly oil and fertiliser, remained relatively subdued and were
generally declining during both episodes.

### Page 15

The inﬂation impact of El Niño is likely to depend as much on
energy prices as on weather conditions themselves. A sustained
decline in oil prices would not only lower fuel inﬂation directly, but
also reduce transport, fertiliser and production costs, helping to
limit second-round eﬀects on food prices.
This matters particularly for economies such as the Philippines,
where food carries a large weight in the CPI basket. Our estimates
suggest a 20% decline in fuel prices could lower headline inﬂation

by around 100bp. Evidence from the current episode suggests a
mixed and less alarming impact across food commodities. While
rice prices have remained relatively contained in both the
Philippines and India, wheat and vegetable oil prices have risen
more noticeably. Importantly, the latter appear to reﬂect
geopolitical disruptions and broader supply constraints as much as
El Niño-related eﬀects.
Food price risks in 2027 nevertheless remain skewed to the upside.

Looking ahead, global wheat production is projected to decline by
around 3% in 2026/27, reﬂecting weaker harvests in major
producers including the US, Australia and Argentina. Rice output is
also expected to decline by 2% in 2026/27, the ﬁrst annual decline
in a decade, as El Niño weighs on harvests in major Asian
exporters. Nevertheless, energy prices are likely to remain the
dominant driver of the inﬂation outlook. Food inﬂation risks could
delay the pace of disinﬂation in some economies, but lower oil

prices should still be the primary force behind headline inﬂation
moderation across most of developing Asia in 2027.
El Nino risks persist, but the inﬂation impact
remains limited and uneven

What does all this mean for central
banks?
If we’re right about fuel prices correcting, most Asian central banks
should gain conﬁdence that inﬂation is moving sustainably back
towards target, opening the door to policy easing in the second half
of 2027 to support growth. Japan remains the key exception. Unlike
elsewhere in the region, its inﬂation is increasingly being driven by
stronger wage growth, labour shortages and ﬁrmer inﬂation

expectations. This should keep the Bank of Japan on a tightening
path for longer than the rest of the region.
Growth slowdown should strengthen the case for rate cuts in
some economies
Real policy rates are near the upper end of historical ranges across
much of Asia, with the notable exceptions of the Philippines and
Taiwan. High real rates matter because they increase borrowing
costs and tend to weigh on consumption, investment and credit
growth.

Growth is already slowing in several economies. Lagged eﬀects of
past monetary tightening could weigh on activity into 2027.
Despite still-negative real rates, the Philippines has seen growth
moderate following an aggressive tightening cycle, while Australia
is likely to face a softer growth backdrop. Higher Australian interest
rates have weighed on household demand and are beginning to
feed through to the housing market. Falling house prices there

could further constrain consumption through negative wealth
eﬀects, while weaker housing activity may weigh on investment

### Page 17

and government revenues. Australia’s labour market has also
started to show clearer signs of cooling.
Against this backdrop, Australia and the Philippines are the two
economies where we see the strongest case for rate cuts in the
second half of 2027 as policymakers shift focus from inﬂation to
supporting growth.
The case for easing is less compelling in North Asia
By contrast, growth in Korea and Taiwan is likely to moderate as the
AI-driven upswing matures and overheating concerns recede. Even

so, inﬂation should remain close to target, and economic activity
should remain relatively resilient. As a result, policy easing is less
urgent than in economies such as Australia and the Philippines.
FX considerations could delay easing in India and Indonesia
FX stability may limit the scope for rate cuts in India and Indonesia.
Unlike some regional peers, the Reserve Bank of India (RBI) has
historically been reluctant to use interest rates to defend the rupee.

However, policymakers recently signalled that rates could help
support currency stability if needed. While India's growth and
inﬂation backdrop should balance better in 2027, with inﬂation
closer to target and growth moderating gradually, FX management
is an important wildcard. Given the relatively shallow hiking cycle,
we don’t expect the RBI to come under signiﬁcant pressure to
reverse course and cut rates.
Indonesia faces a diﬀerent challenge. The rupiah remains one of the

region's most interest-rate-sensitive currencies. We expect it to
remain among Asia’s weakest currencies in 2027. Even if growth
softens and inﬂation stays manageable, Bank Indonesia (BI) may
be more cautious about easing policy. Maintaining a suﬃciently
attractive rate diﬀerential and limiting FX volatility will likely remain
important policy considerations, suggesting BI may lag other
central banks in the easing cycle.
Inﬂation likely to fall close to central bank

targets by 4Q27

Can tech-linked currencies maintain their
leadership in 2027?
FX outlook 2027: Rotation underway, but tech currencies should
continue to lead
Asian currencies have had a mixed run in 2026. AI-linked currencies
such as the KRW, CNY, SGD and MYR generally outperformed, while
the INR, IDR and PHP weakened amid softer growth, higher energy
prices and domestic challenges. One notable exception among the
tech-linked economies is the Taiwanese dollar, which depreciated

by around 5% despite strong current account balances, as a tech-
sector sell-oﬀ in recent months triggered equity outﬂows.
In 2027, lower oil prices and a softer dollar should provide a more
supportive backdrop for Asian FX. However, we think current
account balances and capital ﬂows are likely to play a larger role
than interest-rate diﬀerentials in driving FX performance. While
some rotation into South Asian currencies is already underway, we
continue to expect tech-linked currencies, particularly the SGD in

Southeast Asia and the KRW and TWD in North Asia, to outperform.
This is thanks to strong current account surpluses and exporter FX
conversion, which provide ongoing support. By contrast, currencies
with weaker external positions and less stable capital inﬂows
remain more vulnerable.
Current account surpluses trump rate diﬀerentials in 2027
Rate diﬀerentials are becoming less supportive for EM Asia. Most
Asian central banks are nearing the end of their tightening cycles

and are likely to prepare for policy easing during 2027. Interest-rate

support for Asian currencies should gradually diminish, particularly
for rate-sensitive currencies such as the IDR.
Instead, current account balances and capital ﬂows are likely to
play a larger role in driving FX performance. The CNY and KRW have
been among the strongest performers since the US-Iran conﬂict in
February 2026, appreciating despite a relatively resilient DXY. This
suggests domestic fundamentals are playing a larger role in driving
Asian FX than broad dollar movements.

We remain bullish on the KRW, SGD and TWD. The AI cycle may be
plateauing, but activity remains high. Strong AI-related exports
should continue to support current account surpluses, while
exporter FX conversion should help oﬀset capital outﬂows. In Korea,
sizeable equity outﬂows since September have not prevented KRW
appreciation, highlighting the importance of exporter conversion
and capital repatriation. Measures to encourage domestic
investment and improve shareholder returns could further support

the KRW in 2027. Similarly, Japan's eﬀorts to improve domestic
returns and shareholder distributions could gradually slow capital
outﬂows over time. So, currencies backed by large current account
surpluses, particularly the KRW, TWD and CNY, should be relatively
well supported even as rate diﬀerentials become less favourable.
The main risk to this view is a sharper-than-expected slowdown in
the AI cycle, which could weaken export earnings and reduce

current account support for these currencies.
Rotation towards South Asia should continue, but selectively
Post-midterms, there’s been a rotation from North Asian to South
Asian currencies. The tech sell-oﬀ and lower oil prices have
improved valuations across parts of South Asia. Falling energy
prices should continue to support oil-importing currencies such as
the INR, IDR and PHP.
However, tighter global ﬁnancial conditions mean capital will not be

distributed evenly. We remain more constructive on the INR, as
growth should remain relatively resilient, supported by supply-
chain diversiﬁcation and continued FDI inﬂows. By contrast,

and capital ﬂows playing a larger role.

Will resident demand for foreign assets

continue to constrain Asian FX?
Resident demand for foreign assets remains a key reason why
stronger external balances aren’t translating into sustained Asian
FX appreciation. Current account surpluses remain supportive for
several Asian currencies.
Two structural forces are at work. First, resident demand for foreign
assets continues to rise as retail and institutional investors, along
with corporates, seek greater diversiﬁcation and access to higher

### Page 21

returns abroad. Second, outbound direct investment remains
elevated as ﬁrms reposition supply chains and expand overseas
operations. These trends are structural and unlikely to reverse
quickly.
Taiwan provides a clear example. Corporates continue to diversify
production outside China, with investment increasingly directed
towards the US and Southeast Asia. The sharp rise in outward direct
investment in 2026 reﬂects long-term supply-chain repositioning.

While these outﬂows have weighed on the TWD, the drag could
lessen if the AI cycle moderates in 2026, reducing the need for
overseas capex.
Japan remains another important source of regional capital
outﬂows. Domestic institutional investors, including pension funds
and investment trusts, remain net buyers of foreign assets as part
of long-term portfolio diversiﬁcation strategies. While some life
insurers reduced unhedged foreign bond holdings in August and

September as the yen appreciated, the broader trend remains
overseas allocation. This has been a key constraint on JPY
appreciation. While potential capital repatriation has drawn greater
scrutiny since Tokyo moved to stabilise the currency after its sharp
drop earlier this year, concerns over Japan’s ﬁscal outlook and
government bond supply have dampened appetite for large-scale
inﬂows.
Korea illustrates why capital outﬂows don’t necessarily translate

into FX weakness. Residents (including retail investors and the
National Pension Scheme) continue to invest heavily abroad, with
equity outﬂows annualising at around $100bn. Corporates have
also increased overseas asset accumulation through trade credit
and oﬀshore FX deposits. However, Korea's largely AI-driven current
account surplus and strong exporter FX conversion will likely be
suﬃcient to oﬀset these outﬂows, supporting our KRW
appreciation view.

India has emerged as a growing source of capital outﬂows, with
annual outward FDI more than doubling since 2024 as ﬁrms deploy
capital overseas rather than domestically. While recent data

Strong current account balances can still support currencies,

How vulnerable is Asia to a further rise
in global bond yields?
Asia is not immune, but better positioned than developed markets
Amid the sharp rise in advanced-economy bond yields and the US-
Iran conﬂict, Asian rates have become more sensitive to US rates in
2026. Looking ahead, Asia is likely to remain exposed to higher

global yields as US yields and term premia stay elevated, reﬂecting
worsening ﬁscal positions among major sovereign issuers and
increased debt supply associated with AI-related investment.
Higher global rates would raise debt-servicing costs and put further
pressure on ﬁscal balances across the region. However, Asian
sovereigns are generally better positioned than developed market
peers to absorb a period of higher borrowing costs. Public debt
burdens are typically lower, reliance on foreign investors is generally

Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 23

smaller, and debt maturities are often longer, particularly relative to
Europe. This means higher rates should feed through to
government interest expenses more gradually.
In addition, many Asian economies beneﬁt from strong domestic
savings and deep local investor bases. In several cases, solid
external positions provide an important buﬀer against tighter
global ﬁnancial conditions.
Foreign ownership remains a key vulnerability in some markets
Not all economies are equally insulated. Foreign ownership of

government debt remains relatively high in markets such as
Indonesia and the Philippines, making them more sensitive to shifts
in global risk appetite. As a result, a further rise in global yields
could have a larger impact on domestic bond markets and
ﬁnancing costs in these economies.
Japan is an outlier, but near-term risks remain manageable
Japan remains an outlier in gross public debt, estimated at around
205% of GDP in 2026 by the IMF. However, broader measures that

net out government cash, deposits, and other ﬁnancial assets —
such as loans and debt securities — reduce net debt to roughly
137% of GDP, according to the IMF.
In the near term, Japan retains signiﬁcant buﬀers. The government
bond market is deep and liquid, debt maturities are long, debt is
issued almost entirely in yen, and substantial public-sector assets
provide an additional cushion. These factors should help limit near-
term funding risks even if global yields remain elevated.

The longer-term challenge is diﬀerent. Japan’s rising age-related
spending and higher interest costs will continue to strain public
ﬁnances, making ﬁscal sustainability increasingly dependent on
stronger nominal growth.
Near-term risks to Asia debt remain
manageable

Source: CEIC, IMF
Conclusion
In a capital-scarce world, Asia stands out by attracting a growing
share of global FDI and remaining at the centre of the AI supply
chain, two forces that continue to support the region's external
balances.
However, tighter global ﬁnancial conditions mean capital will
become increasingly selective. In FX, we remain constructive on the
KRW and TWD, where strong AI-related exports should continue to
support current account balances and exporter FX conversion

should help oﬀset capital outﬂows. Measures to encourage
repatriation of overseas assets could provide additional tailwind.
We also favour the INR due to resilient growth, supply-chain
diversiﬁcation, and steady FDI inﬂows.
For rates, moderating inﬂation gives most Asian central banks
scope to ease policy in 2H27, with Australia and the Philippines
oﬀering the strongest cases for rate cuts to support growth. Japan
remains the key outlier. Stronger wage growth and more persistent

inﬂation raise the risk of a more hawkish-than-expected BoJ,
particularly if domestic demand continues to strengthen. The Bank
of Korea, meanwhile, could deliver a surprise cut if the K-shaped
growth dynamic widens and domestic growth weakens further.
Overall, we expect growth leadership to broaden as the AI cycle
matures. While AI-related sectors and currencies should continue to
outperform, lower energy prices, resilient domestic demand and
ongoing supply-chain diversiﬁcation should narrow the gap

between the region's winners and laggards.

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Stronger Growth, Costlier Money
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The latest GDP report and annual revisions show a sturdier U.S. economy than previously
reported. According to the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA), real GDP grew at a 2.2%
annualized pace in Q2 compared to 1.5% previously estimated, and Q1 was revised
from 2.1% to 2.5%. Both 2024 and 2025 were also revised slightly higher. Most of the

first half of the year revision was in categories related to the Artificial Intelligence (AI)
buildout, but real consumer spending has also been revised slightly higher, accelerating
from 0.7% annualized growth in Q1 to 3.8% in Q2 despite surging energy prices and
slow hiring.
Spending in July and August remained much stronger than labor market conditions alone
would suggest, also averaging 3.7% annualized growth. Upside revisions to net interest

income and personal saving suggest households have benefited from more income than
was previously apparent, with personal saving revised higher back to 2023. The new data,
according to the BEA, show the January to July 2026 savings rate averaged 4.8% versus
3.3% prerevision, strengthening consumer spending underpinnings despite moderate
hiring, high energy prices and rising borrowing costs.
The BEA update also included inflation methodological changes related to categories

inflated by the AI boom—such as portfolio management fees and computer software and
accessories. These improved the inflation picture to some extent—August “core” PCE
inflation was reduced by 0.3 ppts to 3% YoY, bringing it closer to “core” consumer price
index inflation, which was 2.5% YoY and 2% on a three-month annualized basis. While still
above the 2% Federal Reserve (Fed) target, “core” PCE now appears stable at 3% year over
year since January rather than accelerating. These methodological changes are expected

to dampen pass-through of AI-related price pressures into “core” inflation and effects of
stock-market swings on inflation.
They also produced an encouraging improvement in recent PCE inflation momentum.
Three-month annualized “core” PCE inflation slowed from 4.3% at the peak of tariff
effects in February to 2.1% in August. For durable goods, this rate was down from 5.8%
in February to 1.5% in August, and, for services ex-energy and healthcare, it slowed from

4.2% to 2.2%, for example. Inflation is volatile on a three-month change basis, so it
remains to be seen whether this moderation continues without further Fed tightening.
For now, the trends in the San Francisco Fed’s cyclical versus acyclical inflation measures
diverged again in August. As expected, given softening wage growth, prices most sensitive
to the labor market have eased further. At 3% YoY, this inflation category is down from
8% in early 2023 and 4.6% two years ago is back to prepandemic levels and is likely

to ease more (Exhibit 1A). In contrast, supply bottlenecks, high energy prices and other
industry-specific factors have kept acyclical inflation on an uptrend. As shown in Exhibit
1B, the relentless increase in freight costs could prolong upside pressure on this category.
The diesel market has tightened in recent months, boosting its price by 70% YoY,
consistent with Brent oil at around $200 per barrel and large reverberations across
a wide swath of goods and services prices. A government-policy-related decline in

available truck drivers is also raising transportation costs more than expected. So are
tight ocean shipping capacity and longer/more expensive voyages due to the U.S./Iran
War. Supply-chain stress also increased as companies rushed to front-run potential new
tariff increases.
On the positive side for the inflation outlook, unit labor costs (ULC) are up modestly
as robust productivity growth has absorbed much of the wage growth, limiting a major
source of domestic inflation. Upside productivity growth revisions are likely to further

reduce ULC, which could help shift focus from energy and AI-related inflation to
eventual disinflationary effects once oil and refined fuel prices start to ease. Sustained
productivity gains would support growth and margins while limiting inflation and the
need for much higher interest rates.
Investment Implications
We favor selective Equity exposure
to companies with durable demand
and pricing power, while remaining
cautious about businesses most

exposed to fuel, freight, and
financing costs. We would also
gradually increase long-duration
exposure.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Surging nominal growth has boosted corporate revenues (up 13% YoY in Q2, according to the
BEA) while contained unit labor costs helped raise margins and profits more than expected.
With labor costs declining as a share of revenues, investment-related tax incentives and
rock-bottom net interest expense, pre-tax nonfinancial corporate profits as measured in GDP
accounts rose 21% YoY in Q2. Total corporate profits after-tax were up 18.2% YoY, according to

the BEA.
Surging AI-related imports and investment are keeping the business and profits cycle on
an uptrend both here and abroad. According to BofA Global Research, the global economic
cycle backdrop improved further in September, as reflected in a broad-based increase in its
Global Wave1 indicator. Broadening U.S. manufacturing strength, fueled by ongoing AI-driven
investment, a healthier orders backlog and low inventories suggest that the expansion has

room to run—a constructive environment for earnings. Typically, a Global Wave upswing is
consistent with rising earnings revisions ratios and equity price uptrends. According to the
Global Wave, global earnings growth expectations remain exceptionally strong.
Still, absent a resolution of war-related energy and transportation disruptions, the same
industrial strength intensifies inflation pressures. A prolonged supply shock raises the risk of
restrictive interest rates and weakening economic growth. In this context, the surge in the

10-year Treasury yield above 5% is starting to reflect more than optimism about U.S. growth
and strengthening productivity outlook. Oil’s tightest recent correlation with 10-year Treasury
yields in 35 years suggests a common energy-price thread behind rising yields in developed
countries.
Bond market spillovers from normalizing Japan yields and capital repatriation are another
common thread. Higher yields on foreign sovereign bonds such as in Japan—which has

escaped stagnation and deflation by intensely pursuing investment-led growth—weaken the
relative appeal of Treasurys. That distinction matters for portfolios. A yield increase driven
mainly by durable productivity gains would come with a stronger outlook for future income
and earnings. A yield increase driven by cost shocks and greater uncertainty raises financing
costs without the same benefit.
In sum, the economy remains more resilient than expected, but the bond market is also

charging more for an uncertain inflation outlook given untethered government debt and
cooling global demand for Treasurys spurred by geopolitical tensions and Japan capital
repatriation. The case for much further tightening becomes stronger if wage growth
strengthens, productivity lags, and unit labor costs reaccelerate, causing cyclical inflation to
also pick up steam. The case becomes weaker if that measure stays contained while fuel and
freight pressures recede or rising Treasury yields reduce asset prices and economic growth.
